ParlaMint-ES_2020-12-01-CD201201.u1	Good morning, ladies and gentlemen. The meeting was resumed. Sections 12, Foreign Affairs, European Union and Cooperation, and 34, Financial Relations with the European Union We continue the debate on the opinion of the draft Law on General State Budgets for 2021. Firstly, it corresponds to the debate in sections 12, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, European Union and Cooperation, and 34, Financial Relations with the European Union. For the presentation of the sections, I have the floor on behalf of the Government, the Minister for Foreign Affairs, the European Union and Cooperation.
ParlaMint-ES_2020-12-01-CD201201.u2	Thank you very much, President. Ladies and gentlemen, I appear to outline the budget lines of my department for 2021, a budget that is commensurate with our ambitions and in line with the current needs of our external action. The Ministry of Foreign Affairs, European Union and Cooperation aspires to a consolidated budget in 2021 of 1852 million euros. This budget includes the resources allocated to the ministry as such, also those allocated to the Spanish Agency for International Cooperation for Development, Aecid, as well as those of the Cervantes Institute. This represents an increase of 17% compared to the base budget 2020. These are new budgets with which this Government proposes two objectives: to directly and effectively meet Spain's interests on the international scene and also to meet the interests and needs of our citizens and companies abroad. To this end, we articulate these budgets around three axes which constitute in this legislature the backbone of our external action and which I am going to describe. First, a commitment determined by multilateralism and regional alliances. The current moment requires strengthening Spain's international position as a driving force in the European Union, as a nodal multilateral actor and also as a builder of alliances with regions such as Latin America, the Mediterranean and Africa. These budgets aim to contribute on this basis to a global response to challenges such as the COVID-19 pandemic, climate change and major global inequalities; multilateralism to manage our interdependence and thus protect our citizens. Multilateral diplomatic action will therefore have a special focus on these budgets. We want to strengthen our position at the United Nations by deepening areas in which Spain has demonstrated international leadership and which I would like to remind you of: Agenda 2030; Women, Peace and Security; combating climate change; feminism; combating hunger; protecting and promoting human rights; combating terrorism; and protecting the victims. We also want to support the global health system and the implementation of the global pacts on refugees and migration, as well as the launch of a national humanitarian diplomacy strategy. Our voluntary contributions to United Nations agencies will be allocated ten times as much as in the previous financial period. Also within this framework we will support the strengthening of the United Nations base in Spain, in Quart de Poblet, Valencia, as a provider of value-added services, technology and communications for all United Nations agencies from Valencia. Secondly, the modernisation and improvement of our services. The budgets represent for my department the opportunity - by incorporating the funds of the European Recovery and Resilience Mechanism - to accelerate the reforms that are already under way. This is particularly the case with digital transformation, especially focused on consular care for the citizen and the digital teaching of Spanish abroad. It is also the way to improve the energy efficiency and sustainability of our buildings. These European funds represent an increase of at least EUR 128 million. The Digitalisation Plan aims to open more efficient management formulas by improving the quality of our consular care and enabling an electronic headquarters to facilitate consular procedures. Our main objective is to give priority and more effective care to the almost 3 million Spaniards living abroad, as well as to the almost 19 million Spaniards who travel abroad each year, and also to the foreign nationals we hope will be able to return soon to Spain. In the area of consular action, EUR 1.9 million will also be allocated for aid under the regular protection of Spanish nationals abroad. Let me recall here the current health emergency, which resulted from March in a real consular crisis in which our Foreign Service assisted thousands of Spanish citizens abroad and contributed to the return of more than 40,000 citizens to our country. The other major project for 2021, together with the above-mentioned digital transformation, is the transfer of all the members of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs to a single headquarters in the square of the Marquis de Salamanca, a building adapted to current needs and better prepared for public attention. They will also invest in the common services of the ministry 21 million euros to improve its functioning and increase its resilience and responsiveness. Thirdly, commitment to development cooperation. International cooperation, in its dual aspect of development cooperation and of cultural promotion and dissemination abroad, will count in the year 2021 with 656 million euros: 293 of them will be managed by the Secretariat of State for International Cooperation for Development, 245 million euros will be managed by the Aecid and 118 million euros, the Cervantes Institute. These budgets are a clear commitment to the 2030 Agenda and official development assistance. This aid from all Spanish public administrations for the next year grows to EUR 3115 million, 20% more than in the 2018 budgets, an increase that puts the official development aid effort at 0.25 per cent of gross national income. We take a step forward in our goal of 0.5% of gross national income at the end of the parliamentary term, which is our commitment. We know that we have to take firm steps in that direction, we know that this year our possibilities are limited, given the crisis caused by COVID, but we send a very clear message of our commitment to move forward in this direction. The contribution of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, 788 million euros, will represent 28% of the official development aid of the General State Administration, an increase of 23% compared to the 2020 budgets. The Secretariat of State for International Cooperation will have a budget of 293 million, with an increase of 62 million euros destined mainly to make contributions to multilateral development organizations in the area of health and food security, the ones most affected by COVID-19. In the area of financial cooperation, the Fund for the Promotion of Development, Fonprode, will have an amount of €200 million and an approved operating limit of €375 million for reimbursable operations. As for the Water and Sanitation Cooperation Fund, by 2021 it will have 15 million euros, with an approval limit of 65 million euros. Aecid will have €384 million in transfers and other revenue, 15% more than in the 2020 budget. And I also highlight cooperation operations delegated by the European Commission in the amount of EUR 32 million. Aecid will channel one more year of calls for civil society to join development cooperation: at least EUR 31.5 million guaranteed, plus the proportional share of the 0.7 % effective collection for other social purposes. The ministry will also continue to support foundations that work closely with Spanish cooperation, such as the Carolina Foundation and the International and Ibero-American Foundation for Public Administration and Policy. With regard to humanitarian aid, the increase in the national budget will make it possible to fulfil the commitment to increase humanitarian aid up to 10% of official development aid in Aecide. As far as the Cervantes Institute is concerned, this is, as you all know, a strategic institution for cultural promotion and dissemination abroad, and for that reason it will have a budget of 174 million euros. It represents an increase of 50 million euros, mainly due to the planned contribution of the Recovery Mechanism, which will allow the technological transformation of the Cervantes Institute to boost the digital teaching of Spanish. Transfers from the State to the Cervantes Institute also grow by 4.2 million euros - the largest experienced in recent years - and with this the institute will be able to open its first headquarters in Africa - in Dakar, Senegal - and in Los Angeles, as is its intention. These budgets will satisfy, of course, the payroll of the 1,172 employees assigned to the central services that this ministry plans to have in 2021 and those of the 4683 employees abroad. Also, logically, the maintenance of our external network, consisting of one hundred and eighteen embassies, ninety consular offices and ten permanent representations, which will absorb 369 million euros. In this regard, I note that, although no significant increases in the current expenditure on goods and services of our external representations have been foreseen, in 2021 we will expand the external network with the opening of a new Consulate General in the Chinese city of Chengdu. I ended my speech by stating that the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, European Union and Cooperation also aims to have a greater strategic approach, which will lead us to strengthen the Secretariat of State for Global Spain. We want to improve the positioning of Spain in the world. We want to boost economic diplomacy to support the internationalization of our businesses - in particular small and medium-sized ones - and to attract investment, as well as to make a better contribution to the major debates of the future. We will devote EUR 13 million to this. In short, today I bring before your ladies and gentlemen the financial expression of an ambitious and modern external action and of a diplomatic and consular deployment that wants to rise to the level of the moment we are here. Finally, ladies and gentlemen, this is the draft budget with which the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, European Union and Cooperation hopes to be able to respond to the challenges of our country on the international scene in 2021 and for which I would like to ask for all my support from here. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

It is an exemplary response in southern Europe, which launches a message that will also be translated into foreign policy. A foreign policy that is going to be based on multilateralism, on the defence of peace, on the defense of feminism and on the defence of human rights; that is, quite contrary to what the Caligula governments have despised so much Trump or Bolsonaro during these years and which, unfortunately, has had enough embolism in this very House. And that's going to mean a lot because defending a peace policy means refusing to support any warlike adventure to which any foreign power wants to take us, thus honoring what was said in Article 6 of the 1931 Republican Constitution, which established that Spain renounced war as an instrument of public policy. And when we say that we are going to defend a policy of international solidarity we are talking about a policy of cooperation that defends that if we ask for vaccines and access to basic medicines for ourselves, we also have to ask for it for all the peoples of the world who need it; defending a policy of solidarity means defending a migration policy based on respect for human rights and international legality by refusing to secondary, as has just happened in France, the demagogic scoundrels defended by the extreme right who would only want to confront - yes, Mr Espinosa - the poor against the poor and the last against the penultimate in order to hide who are really responsible for this crisis. Defending this solidarity policy forces us not to abandon our sisters and brothers in the Sahara, in Palestine, the human rights defenders in Colombia and in Chile who are being threatened and killed by it. It obliges us to demand real respect from our companies and international trade that is not only about marketing for human rights and for labour and environmental standards when they act beyond our borders, and above all it forces us to carry on something that the right-wing usually overlooks; a foreign policy based on republican values of honesty in the exercise of public office. We told Congresswoman Uriarte a few days ago and we repeat it today: we are many and many Republican and non-Republican people who have witnessed constant investigations and allegations of corruption against the Emeritus King and members of the Royal House. For us - those of us who are also concerned about the rule of law - these revelations offend the working citizens and also cause indisputable damage to the external image of Spain, which here some do not care so much. And many of us are clear that we cannot shut up about that either, because neither Riego nor Mariana Pineda were silenced in front of Fernando VII, because Pi i Margall, who was also a deputy in front of Isabel II, did not shut up in front of Alfonso XIII, because in front of Unamuno and Pérez Galdós, who, in addition to being a great Republican, was the brightest Spanish-language writer of the 19th century, and that is why we will not shut up either. And if they put us in the position of having to choose, indeed, between a monarchy constantly questioned by corruption and respect for the principle of the rule of law, respect for the principle of equality before the law and respect for the principle of popular sovereignty, do not hesitate, ladies and gentlemen; between one thing and another we will be very clear, just as the great Benito Pérez Galdós and so many others were clear; let the monarchy perish but let the principles be saved. [[Pause.-A cleaning service worker proceeds to disinfect the speaker's rostrum]] Section 13, Justice We continue with the debate in section 13, Ministry of Justice. For the section's presentation, the Minister of Justice has the floor on behalf of the Government.
ParlaMint-ES_2020-12-01-CD201201.u24	Thank you very much, President. Good morning, ladies and gentlemen. I appear before this House to present to you the budget estimates of the Ministry of Justice contained in the draft Law on General Budgets of the State for 2021. During my speech, I will present the outline of section 13, addressing the most prominent items within each of its chapters and make a brief reference to the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism, as expressed in programme 110.A of these budgets. Before I begin my presentation, however, let me express the importance of these budgets for us to move forward. We have to push for the transformation of the country. We cannot afford to enter European recovery funds without adequate legal and structural support in order to do so. The Budget Bill of 2021 is the most expansive of recent years and contains the largest social spending in a decade. These are budgets designed to contribute to social but also economic recovery, in the face of the crisis caused by COVID-19, and in particular the public accounts that we need to strengthen the public justice service and undertake the structural reforms that our justice needs. They include a consolidated budget of the Ministry of Justice and its agencies amounting to EUR 2144 million, 7.5% more than in the previous year. State expenditure on public justice policies for 2021, with a total budget of EUR 2048 million, amounts to 7.6%, an additional EUR 144.3 million compared to the base budget for the previous year. This means that the allocation levels of all the programmes in Section 13 will be exceeded if we take into account the historical series of the last ten years. We are therefore faced with an appropriate budget to initiate a process of complete transformation of the public justice service, consistent with the 2030 Agenda and the Sustainable Development Goals, but governed by a principle of responsible and realistic implementation. As I have said on more than one occasion, we must not only spend beyond where it is necessary, but also spend better, and we will do so among all, following a model of co-governance between the State and the various autonomous communities with transfers in the field of justice. From the amount I have indicated, the participation of the Ministry of Justice in justice policies - including here the Centre for Legal Studies - reaches the sum of 1975 million, which means an increase of 136 million euros over the previous year. For its part, the participation of the General Council of the Judiciary is 73 million euros, with an increase of 8 million euros, i.e. 12%. Justice, therefore, is progressing and shaping itself as an essential public service, whose development and transformation will be promoted through the new 110.A programme, designed to articulate the European Union's Recovery and Resilience Mechanism. First of all, I wish to introduce the main budget items in our section 13. I'll start with your chapter 1. The overall staffing of chapter 1, staff costs, is 1604 million. This amount exceeds 6,6 %, the previous year's credit. The departure for staff at the service of the Administration of Justice increases by approximately 99 million, mainly due to the shock plan that we have launched and that we are currently deploying. The amount of the plan for 2021 is 43.78 million. The remainder of the increase corresponds to the salary increase agreed by the Government for staff members. With regard to the judicial and prosecutorial career in a context such as the current one, the call for 2021 of 240 new places of entry for judges and prosecutors is, believe me, a great news. We must maintain a stable pace of creation that we hope can continue and, if necessary, increase in the future. With regard to staff in the Administration, the overall system of free access and internal promotion totals 1,452 positions. I would like to point out that the proposal for vacancies represents 100 per cent of the troop replacement rate and an additional 10 per cent is foreseen by 2021, as justice is included in areas requiring troop reinforcement. Therefore, I can convey the great news that the difference between the number of fixed employees, who ceased to serve in each of the fields and bodies during 2019, and the number of fixed employees who joined them is covered. Ladies and gentlemen, I am particularly proud of the effort that these budgets make in Chapter 1 of the justice sector. Human capital is essential so that the provision of this essential, historically under-funded public service can lead us to the future. Moving on to chapter 4. For this chapter, current transfers, the budget amounts to EUR 65.03 million, representing an increase of 11.2%. Of the projects consolidated in this chapter, 75.6% of the budget in this chapter is free legal aid. The benefit of free justice underpins one of the pillars of the rule of law, such as accessibility. We must therefore congratulate ourselves on the effort that is being made in this area. I also warn you that the Government is working on a number of legislative initiatives that will further expand this important social right, which will lead to slight budget increases in future years. We can do more as a society to ensure everyone's access to justice, and we are doing so. Moving on to chapter 6. On the side of the real investments covered in Chapter 6, there is an increase of EUR 22.5 million, largely as a result of the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism. In total there are 194.95 million, of which 62% will be devoted to the digitisation of justice, approximately EUR 120 million, and 24%, i.e. 47 million, to infrastructures, equipment in court buildings, judicial offices and, of course, to the toxicological institute or institutes of legal medicine. I am pleased to inform you at this point that today the Council of Ministers will approve the creation of thirty-three new judicial units for next year. As you know, this is a mandate from this House to the Government contained in Law 3/2020, and we have fulfilled it within the prescribed time frame. Ladies and gentlemen, this government is fulfilling its obligations. With regard to structural reforms, I make special mention of the project to transform the Civil Registry with 12.3 million euros, which will implement a civil registry model based on people, public and free, fully digital and with full participation of the autonomous communities. It is worth highlighting the impetus to the technological development necessary for Law 20/2011 to be a reality through Dicireg. Ladies and gentlemen, since the adoption of the new Civil Registration Act in 2011, this House has had to agree year after year extensions for its entry into force, a democratic anomaly and also an institutional failure that these budgets come to repair. Already in February, I committed myself at this headquarters to promote the transformation of the Civil Registry at once. These budgets confirm and support this commitment. In organizational terms, as you know, we propose starting from 431 general offices in the localities that are party leader. Therefore, it is a project with which the 431 courts of instance that we hope to implement through the new law of organizational efficiency that we have discussed before are easily aligned. This will obviously be complemented by the judicial offices in the municipalities. Digital transformation is one of the main thrusts of the justice project. It is, therefore, the main lever for process improvement and has a meaning reflected in these budgets. The overall budget for digital transformation is now EUR 10.49 million, an increase from 2020 of 2.1%. Here the importance of the creation of the Directorate-General for Digital Transformation, which will articulate and manage all this investment, is highlighted. The technological transformation of the administration of justice and the prosecutor's office within the 112.A program, which for us is tremendously relevant, has a total investment of 102.09 million euros. At this point, the digitisation of the judicial bodies to which we will allocate €72.4 million covers the most important item, including the improvement of procedural management systems and associated systems. Here, for example, they highlight the updating of the technological bases of the application systems for the amount of EUR 35 million, aimed at a sustainable electronic judicial record; the complete updating of audiovisual services of view rooms, with EUR 4 million, renewing the infrastructures and establishing a system of video recording and textualization of recordings with automatic transcription systems; the centre of technological attention to judicial users with EUR 7.5 million, in which we will make progress with the Connecta Justicia programme. We will also complete the implementation of the digital justice solution in the National Court and the Supreme Court, with 1.2 million euros. With regard to the digitisation of prosecutions, EUR 15 million is planned to address improvements in the technology centre for tax users, the completion of the digital prosecution, improvements in the applications of the prosecution and in the training of users of digital prosecution services. I shall now turn to the consolidation of electronic communications, with EUR 7.4 million. The main focus of the item is on improving and consolidating these communications, such as LexNET, Hermes, Electronic Judicial Headquarters and the General Access Point of the Administration of Justice. Ladies and gentlemen, for all these projects, EUR 7.1 million, 9.2% more than the previous year, will be allocated in the sub-secretariat area to investments in new equipment and programme licences, which will lead to better service delivery. Finally, the appropriations allocated to this chapter also include investment in infrastructure and equipment, for which an amount of EUR 47 million is foreseen. The appropriations allocated in this regard will provide the basis for the deployment of an ambitious programme of sustainable headquarters of the Administration of Justice for the years 2022 and 2023, including the design and implementation of the new judicial offices in the municipalities. This expenditure is of vital importance, not only for the proper provision of the public justice service, but also as an element of the vertebration of the State through the infrastructures of justice. In addition to the appropriations already mentioned in Chapter 6 for works, this Ministry will have the financing of energy transition projects and for decarbonization in judicial and administrative offices, through the credits of the State Energy Secretariat of the Ministry of Ecological Transition and Demographic Challenge from the European Union Recovery and Resilience Fund. I turn to chapter 7. This chapter, for appropriations for capital transfers, has a budget allocation of EUR 8 million. This is a credit that is entirely dedicated to internal transfers, mainly are transfers to the Generalitat of Catalonia -7 297 990 euros - by the judicial bodies put into operation between the years 2018, 2019 and 2020. I shall now refer to programme 110.A, that is, the mechanism in which the whole system of recovery and resilience is inserted. As you have seen, these are the transformation budgets of the Justice Administration, and this is what this 110.A programme points out, which will allow us to start programming structural reforms in a co-governance scheme. They will agree with me that it opens up more than a window of opportunity with the decisive impetus of Europe for the quality of our public justice service. The budget allocated specifically to justice is EUR 410 million, spread over three years, involving structural reforms, quality of justice public service and rule of law. The 110.A programme, to which 27.74 million euros will be allocated in 2021, will allow during the next year to promote the technological measures of the zero project of Justice 2030 that were agreed with the autonomous communities for the management of the pandemic and digital mediation, jobs relocated and pre-date. These three technological measures are essential, and they are essential for health security against COVID, and require a final push for implementation. The year 2021 must end with the significant territorialization of these measures of the zero project, in a coordinated manner with the autonomous communities with transferred competences. This will not only ensure the continuation of the public service of justice throughout the territory in the face of possible changes in the health situation, but - and this is very important - we will also have managed to implement what will be de facto the digital infrastructures of the new architecture of the Administration of Justice - an architecture that will be regulated in the following legal instruments, of which you have already heard me speak: the preliminary draft law on procedural efficiency of the public service of justice, the preliminary draft law on organizational efficiency of the public service of justice and, finally, the preliminary draft law on digital efficiency of the public service of justice. In this way, ladies and gentlemen, we can already concentrate the execution of the bulk of the funds of the 110.A programme, EUR 382 million, over the next 24 months of the 2022s and 2023s, and do so in a realistic and fully coordinated manner. I conclude, ladies and gentlemen, by transferring something you have heard me say on other occasions. The success of our projects will depend on our musculature in design, development, project implementation, and accountability. Therefore, what we have ahead is a great challenge, a great challenge that we can achieve by co-governing and co-responsibility; an opportunity for justice that we cannot miss, and I can assure you that we will not. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]
ParlaMint-ES_2020-12-01-CD201201.u25	Thank you very much, Minister. My pride, therefore, in being a Minister of Defence, in representing the men and women who are currently in active service or reserve in the Armed Forces, those who, being retired, comply with the principle of political neutrality and, of course, my lack of recognition for those who defend themselves, who are, I would say, imbued with the military uniform, which they no longer have the right to wear, to involve the king in his majesty. [[Applauds]] If we look exclusively at the budget of Section 14 in the current financial year, the defence budget will be provided with appropriations amounting to EUR 9411.93 million. Resources thus grow by EUR 417 million, 4.64% compared to 2020. Including the self-governing agencies and the National Intelligence Centre, the consolidated budget amounts to 10,499 million euros, without accounting for the expansion of appropriations for peace mission operations. I am interested in qualitatively analysing the aims of these budgets and how we will materialise their objectives through the breakdown of the main chapters. Let me begin, logically, with chapter 1, staff costs, which devotes several lines of action to staff resources, which is the most valuable capacity of the Armed Forces. The appropriations allocated amount to EUR 4928.16 million, representing an increase of EUR 175.53 million and representing 52.4 million of the total defence budget. The objective for which we will continue to work is to achieve excellence through an effort in training and selection that will enable each post to be filled with the most suitable people, and that is an irrevocable objective, as it is to continue with the improvement of the living conditions of military personnel, especially those of troops and navy. We will also continue to support reconciliation and equality policies, which are fundamental to achieving real and lasting progress. These credits - and I care very much to highlight this part - will enable us to meet all the salaries of the department's staff as well as to take a first step in a process of reviewing the salaries of military personnel. This initiative includes updating the salaries agreed with public employees and the instrument programmed in the staffing tables for next year. In particular, let me tell you, as regards the concrete implementation of the EUR 175 million increase in Chapter 1, that we are going to make a special reference - something that is important to us - to a structural improvement relevant to the troops and navy. The change in the basic level of the destination supplement from the current 13th to the 14th is a structural change that has permanent retributive consequences, as will the wage increases that, immediately after these budgets are approved - and we will try to make it on 1st January - will be made to the lowest salaries of troops and navy of the Armed Forces, which are those who receive lower wages. We will immediately get to work so that these increases can be made effective as of 1 January, once we do the appropriate administrative processing. In addition, today's Council of Ministers agrees, on a proposal from the Ministry of Defence, to increase the availability supplement for all military personnel on the reserve, which will thus cease to see this concept cut by 20% in their salaries when they move on to this situation of availability. A very important but fair effort is being made, and they will be able to perceive it fully with effect from 1 November 2020. I would also like to stress that it is expected that 3,500 soldiers and sailors will be incorporated into the Army and the Navy in January 2021, and new selection cycles with an anticipated offer of 6,500 positions will be published throughout 2021. A significant effort has also been projected in the number of places offered for the ratio of permanent services, reaching the 1000 places planned for 2020. With regard to civilian personnel, the recently approved Royal Decree 936/2020 of 27 October, which incorporates the offer of public employment, includes a major special effort for the defence hospital network. In this current crisis situation by the pandemic and as a result of lessons learned from Operation Balmis and Mission Baluarte, we will improve military health resources. To our military health professionals, our infinite thanks for all the work they have done and are doing with absolute generosity and defense of our values. [[Applauds]] To finance the training of Armed Forces personnel in the forty-five military centres, a total of EUR 439.69 million will be devoted, an increase of 2%. In particular, we will make a major investment in education, specialization and work guidance for troops and seafarers, which will be increased by EUR 2 million to strengthen the Consue+ and Directions for Employment programmes. We also want to address what was our political commitment to reconciliation policies and to improving the living conditions of the men and women of the Armed Forces. We will increase - what I said in my first intervention - the places in child education to facilitate the reconciliation of family and military life of our men and women. [[Applauds]] It will be increased from twenty-five to twenty-nine child education centres, with 294 new places in child education, employing resources of €5.51 million. The second increase will be EUR 25.4 million directly related to the living conditions of our professional military personnel in the field of communication so that they can improve, by this ease in communications, their personal and family relations. With the current expenditures, ladies and gentlemen, on the goods and services of chapter 2, the objective is to maintain an operational, fully prepared and trained Armed Forces; an Armed Forces capable of sustaining the deployment of the forces necessary to maintain both its permanent missions and commitments to international organizations and the defence of national interests abroad. The appropriations available for 2021 amount to EUR 895.6 million, representing 9.5% of the total budget and an increase of EUR 75.95 million. The so significant reduction accumulated in the last ten years - I am not going to blame anyone here, because I think we all have a sense of state - forced to prioritize the training and enlistment of the assigned units to integrate into the core of the joint force and into operations abroad. We are aware that the 2008 levels are not yet reached, but we believe that these budgets help to restrict the trend in this direction. I would like to highlight the budgetary reflection of peacekeeping operations, and with regard to chapter 2, there is an increase in the provision for peacekeeping operations to meet the financial needs of our units abroad. With regard to the resources in Chapter 4, which covers current transfers, financial contributions to the international security agencies to which Spain is a party are financed. Because Spain is a strong, serious and reliable ally in all its international commitments, primarily the European Union and NATO. And we also include in these expenses what refers to the INTA autonomous body, the National Intelligence Centre and some of the training expenses. Finally, ladies and gentlemen, with the resources of Chapter 6, dedicated to real investments, the objective is to obtain and develop the defence capabilities necessary to confront the current strategic context. We also managed to support the Spanish defence industry, so that Spain would remain at the forefront of technology, while helping to boost the economy and the national productive base and ensure self-resiliency. It is necessary to equip our men and women with the Armed Forces, our most precious asset, with the best means available to carry out their missions and, at the same time, to contribute to the creation of jobs, as we are doing in many areas of Spain. The appropriations available in this chapter amount to EUR 3090 million, equivalent to 32.8% of the Ministry's total budget. These figures are not the result of predetermined figures in previous years, which are modified in the light of budgetary availability, but, as everyone knows, defence planning is envisaged on broad horizons of one or more legislatures. We are particularly interested in modernisation plans, with an increase of EUR 177.1 million, 8.2% compared to 2020, to tackle the growth of financial commitments made for these programmes in previous years. As I pointed out earlier, these programmes are essential for maintaining the operational capacity of our Armed Forces, providing credibility to national defence and helping to ensure the safety of our men and women and of all Spanish society, creating jobs. Although implemented with Section 14 funds, these programmes serve the objectives of defence policy, but also contribute to those of other State policies, as I said earlier, employment, industrial or foreign action. They also provide technological independence to Spain and Europe and, therefore, it is necessary to highlight the effort made to increase the amount of research, development and technological research in the field of defence, which in 2021 represents 30.44 million euros, an increase of 7.96 million euros. Finally, I would like to point out the presence in this project of Programme 120.A, of the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism, which, within the policy of defence spending, collects the credits allocated to this ministry from the European Union's recovery funds, allocating 75 million euros to promote digital transformation. I shall conclude, ladies and gentlemen. It is clear that the budgets are conditioned by the economic context of the crisis, but they serve us to make a commitment determined by the recovery and to give Spain the role it deserves internationally. These are budgets that advance in improving the condition of the lives of the men and women of our Armed Forces. We want our military to have the highest levels of security, keeping the operation of our Armed Forces intact. These budgets follow the path of the National Security Strategy and the National Security and Defence Policy directives and set out a change of trend and a solid foundation for the future. In short, the budgets of the Ministry of Defence will enable us to work to respond to the objectives of the defence policy; a defence policy that aims to protect the whole of Spanish society and its Constitution, which is, like all its institutions - the monarchy included - a heritage absolutely of all Spanish citizens and for which we will continue to work in defense of the values and freedoms that this Constitution brings and that our Armed Forces, with generosity, surrender and with absolute sense of love for Spain and the citizens, are developing every day. We are proud of our Constitution, our Armed Forces and the role that their men and women play in Spain in the service of citizens, our Constitution and democratic values. From this line that always follows this generous action of the Armed Forces, of those who respect political neutrality and who have no temptation to fall into their past and in military uniforms, it is of which I, like the Minister of Defence, am so proud. I am grateful in advance for the support of the parliamentary groups for the adoption of these budgets. Let us never forget that defence policy is a state policy. This government has always worked on this, and I hope that all the forces that are called the state and that want Spain and its Constitution will work on that. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2020-12-01-CD201201.u49	Thank you, Mr. President. Ladies and gentlemen, I am speaking to your ladies and gentlemen to present the budget section for the ministry whose leadership I was entrusted with by the President of the Government on 13 January. I would therefore like to begin by thanking all 170,000 public employees of the Ministry for the work they have done on the front line during the health crisis in our country and, also, Mr President, to show my appreciation to the members of the State Security Forces and Bodies who have lost their lives as a result of the COVID-19. Ladies and gentlemen, I will begin this speech by making a brief assessment of the budget allocated to the Ministry of the Interior for 2021. We are faced with a budget which, as it cannot be otherwise, is aligned with the main objectives of the State's General Budgets, which are nothing more than an economic revival, the digital and ecological transition, the creation of employment and also the strengthening of public services and territorial cohesion and the promotion of effective equality between men and women. They are public accounts, ladies and gentlemen, committed to the United Nations 2030 Agenda; public accounts that constitute the encrypted and accounting expression of security recovery as a public good; public accounts that are undoubtedly the first deployment of the 2030 Security model, which this ministry wants to develop during this parliamentary term. Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, the budget of the Ministry of the Interior, excluding the autonomous bodies, amounts to EUR 8 906 736 290, which implies an increase of 15.90% in relation to the last budgets approved in these General Courts. This budget makes the Ministry of the Interior the fourth of the Government departments in terms of investment and expenditure. It is an appropriate budget to move forward on the broad lines of the legislature that I had the honour of presenting last February to the Congressional Committee on the Interior. Ladies and gentlemen, I will move forward with the main aspects of the budget following the outline of broad lines based on three major areas of action and nine major axes which form the model I was talking about, Security 2030. It is a model that is born with the firm vocation to ensure the different environments in which citizens develop their lives. The physical, territorial environment, we could say, will also be better protected by measures such as the more than 7 million euros to continue in the modernization of the border perimeter of Ceuta and Melilla, having invested today, in the last two years, in that modernization of the border perimeter, an amount of 23 million euros, which are completed with these more than 7 million euros. Also, the continuation, with more than 12 700 000 euros by 2021, of the process of boosting and improving the capacities of the Asylum and Refugee Office that we have been developing in recent years in line with the increase in its staff, which has multiplied by five the number of troops since June 2018, when the Socialist Party - first, and later, with Unidad Podemos - acceded to the Government. This office will also be strengthened with a further EUR 650 000 for the new comprehensive management system for international protection applications. I should also point out the strengthening of police cooperation with countries of origin and transit to combat the mafia trafficking in persons, which by 2021 will amount to EUR 43 550 000. Within the sphere of space, there is also the secure digitization, commitment of this Government and uniquely of the ministry that I have the honour to lead. It will also be a preferred area of action for exercise 2021. The budget of the Ministry of the Interior will provide €4,218,700 for various projects relating to the renovation of the ministry's technological stock, but also for the digital transformation of the ministry. In this regard, as the senior posts in the Committee on Budgets have already anticipated in this House, the main innovation will be the impetus for the project of the new electronic DNI, for which EUR 6 250 000 will be allocated as part of a wider project with EUR 25 million for the biennium 2021-2023. Mr President, the modernisation of the National Civil Protection and Emergency System and the improvement of its capabilities in emergency and vulnerability situations that we are seeing recently will be reflected in the increase in the budget to nearly EUR 15 million. Progress towards the eradication of the road violence in our society will also be reflected in the increase of more than 7% in the budget of the Directorate-General for Traffic, which with 822 million euros will be the largest in the last nine years and which will include measures such as the following. Firstly, there is a commitment to investment in road safety disclosure and information, which will be provided with the sum of EUR 13 400 000. There is also the 13% increase in Chapter 1 to strengthen the pool of examiners, which has increased from 734 in June 2018 to 802 and which is pending to incorporate 40 examiners of the offer of public employment and 50 for internal promotion, to which will be added in the coming year another 35 of the offer of public employment and also 50 for internal promotion. I refer to this point because of the concern expressed by various parliamentary groups and by the Socialist Group itself and the Government in such a sensitive matter for many Spanish citizens. Secondly, there is an increase in Chapter 2 by 45% to improve - we continue with the Directorate-General for Traffic - the digitisation of procedures in the Directorate-General for Traffic, renewing equipment and processes, but also the application you will already know, miDGT. Finally, Chapter 6 incorporates a 60% increase aimed at strengthening and increasing the mechanisms for monitoring and controlling traffic regulations: 2300 radars, to which 75 will be added next year, 28 new drones and 216 high-definition cameras for seat belt control or mobile use. It is never done for a purpose - I always like to say it - collection, but to prove the fulfillment of something as important as responsible conduct in the field of road safety. Ladies and gentlemen, the second major area of action relates to ensuring coexistence. These budgets make it possible to make steady progress in this direction. Firstly, in Chapter 1, it increases by 3.3%, with an increase of more than EUR 231 million, with more agents of the State Security Forces and Bodies, after seven years in which the governments of the People's Party - as we have repeatedly recalled - cut down 12,000 troops. The public employment offer of this year 2020 has been of 5540 police and civilian guard positions - as did the previous 2019 - and the system of prison institutions is strengthened, since with 1054 new places increases its budget for staff by 9.52% compared to the budget of 2018. But there are also other important measures. Firstly, more than EUR 13.5 million is proposed for compensation and aid to victims of terrorism and for the inauguration in 2021 of the Centre for the Memory of Victims of Terrorism in the city of Vitoria-Gasteiz. It is also included in Chapter 6 - which is increased by 22.4% - a total of 10 800 000 euros for the reinforcement of the Gibraltar Field Plan, in which we have invested more than 40 million euros since July 2018, of which we are already seeing its results and which has allowed the State to recover the initiative against organized crime. At this time, I would also like to thank the Security Forces and Bodies that are implementing that Gibraltar Plan in really difficult situations, but with an absolutely positive result and worthy of recognition. These budgets also involve improving the AlertCops application, strengthening the resources of the National Office for the Fight against Hate Crimes and the ROCA teams of the Civil Guard, or plans for vulnerable groups, such as the Major Security Plan, all of which are covered by the more than €305,000 that integrates the specific budget of the Secretariat of State for Security for this purpose. Not to mention the increase, of course, of personnel and material and personal means for the UFAM units of the Police and Emume of the Civil Guard to fight effectively against the macho violence. For the continuous improvement of the formation of the State Security Forces and Bodies, 4 million euros are also envisaged for the start-up of the university center of the National Police and more than 433 000 euros for the new lines of action of the University Center of the Civil Guard. Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, none of the measures forming part of the 2030 Security model would be possible without providing the extraordinary human team of this ministry with decent working and remuneration conditions and without adequate protection measures in the face of this situation that we are going through, that is, without giving effect to the third major area of action of this department which is nothing other than that of generating public value. Mr President, I have referred to the recovery of the troops cut off by the People's Party in State Security Forces and Bodies, also in Prison Institutions. At this point, I would like to recall that, together with the increase in the number of staff, Chapter 1 incorporates the completion of the wage-equalization agreement, which, with the approval of the third tranche, was launched this year and which will definitively consolidate a total of EUR 807 million in the State General Budgets on the payroll of the National Police and Civil Guard officers, which have increased by 20%. I would like to remind you that you are already charging the third tranche with effect from 1 January of this year. Nor do I want to forget the infrastructure with some data. The Infrastructure Plan 2019-2025 for the State Security Forces and Bodies, for the Civil Guard and National Police, is endowed with an amount of 600 million euros according to the agreement of the Council of Ministers of January 2019, which will entail an investment in the budgets of 2021 of 42.8 million in prison infrastructure and 90.4 million, as I have said, in units of the Civil Guard and National Police. There is also a 60% increase in Chapter 6, investments by the Directorate-General for Traffic, for the rehabilitation, reform and improvement of the premises of the Civil Guard Traffic Group and provincial traffic chiefs and more than EUR 10,668,000 in plans of the Secretariat of State for Security, among other measures. Nor do I want to forget the renovation of work equipment, such as the acquisition by 2021 of more than 5800 new bulletproof vests or the increase in investment in transport elements, which, with a growth of more than 90%, will allow the National Police to renew the vehicles on their own, from a combined perspective of safety and of course environmental sustainability. Or, for example, the new 520 light vehicles and coaches to be purchased within the chapter of the Civil Guard's real investments, which grows by almost 10% to EUR 43 300 000. I do not want to end without reminding you that to the ordinary investment of the department we must add the investment that will be generated within the framework of the Plan of Recovery, Transformation and Resilience, which will include in our case and for the moment various actions in the field of the digital transition. I remember the momentum of the Digital Identity project, with an investment planned for the year 2021 of EUR 6 250 000, within a three-year plan with a total amount of EUR 25 million. To this will be added other lever projects managed in a coordinated way throughout the General Administration of the State, in the fields of digitization, ecological transition and justice from which the Ministry of the Interior will benefit. I'm finishing up, Mr. President. And I do so by reiterating the alignment of the budget of the Ministry of the Interior with the main objectives of the General Budgets of the State, recalling that they are a necessary and essential step for the implementation of the model of Security 2030, based on ensuring the environment, ensuring coexistence and generating public value, as well as on the further development in our country of a concept of human security that retains its reactive capacity, but that incorporates preventive, anticipatory elements and has in people its centre of action. These more than €89 billion are the encrypted and accounting expression of the commitment of this government and this minister to the recovery of security as a public good. Thank you very much. [[Pause.-A cleaning service worker proceeds to disinfect the speaker's rostrum]] Section 17, Transport, Mobility and Urban Agenda We move on to the debate in section 17, Ministry of Transport, Mobility and Urban Agenda. For the presentation of the section, I have the floor, on behalf of the Government, the Minister for Transport, Mobility and Urban Agenda.
ParlaMint-ES_2020-12-01-CD201201.u71	Thank you very much, President. Ladies and gentlemen, I appear before this House to present this draft budget of the Ministry of Transport, Mobility and Urban Agenda for 2021. As you all know, the preparation of the General Budgets of the State and, also, their implementation during the next financial year are framed in an exceptional context, often repeated in this House, as a result of the crisis caused by COVID-19. This crisis is particularly intense in certain essential sectors, such as transport, which is part of the Ministry's competence. With these budgets, the Government wants to promote the economic recovery of our country by alleviating the negative effects of the pandemic and, at the same time, to face the challenge of advancing the establishment of a new growth model based on strengthening the welfare state, the commitment to the knowledge economy, digital transformation and the ecological transition. Since the beginning of this legislature, in the ministry we are working to give a new approach to our lines of action which, as it cannot be otherwise, has its reflection on the budgets that we present to you today. This new approach has two key lines: the reorientation of housing policy to reach those most in need and to improve the sustainability and quality of life in our cities, and the reorientation of mobility policy to make it more inclusive, secure, sustainable and digital. To this end, in the pursuit of these objectives, the current budget proposal, without abandoning the optimization and continuous improvement of our infrastructure network, focuses on investment in mobility and housing. We place the citizen at the centre of our decisions and give a transcendental role to the sustainability and digitization of cities and municipalities that aspire to be more and more efficient. It is precisely these principles that are reflected in the department's two major strategic documents for the next decade: the Safe, Sustainable and Connected Mobility Strategy 2030 and the Spanish Urban Agenda. [[The lady]] On the basis of these premises, I then go on to detail some specific data on the ministry's budget for 2021. The consolidated budget of the Ministry group for 2021 of Chapters 4, 6, 7 and 8 and investments of the entities amounts to 16,664 million euros, an increase of 53 % compared to the currently existing extended budgets. These figures allow us to present the most ambitious budgets of the last decade, budgets with which the Ministry of Transport, Mobility and Urban Agenda wants to contribute in a remarkable way to the economic recovery of the country by increasing the investment and the impulse to the transformation of the Spanish economy from an environmental, social and productivity point of view. A very large part of the resources incorporated into these budgets - almost EUR 5 billion - come from the European funds of the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism. These European resources will be used to finance the projects of two of the key levers of this mechanism: the so-called resilient infrastructures and ecosystems and the urban and rural agenda for territorial cohesion. We estimate that with these budget increases we are going to help our country's GDP increase next year by EUR 8780 million and create 112 200 new jobs, which together represents a percentage contribution to GDP of 0.8 points. The will of the ministry is that these should not be an annual budget anymore, but should be the beginning of a real transformation of the economic and social reality of our country. To this end, as I have pointed out, we are promoting ten-year strategies based on the reorientation of housing and mobility policies, in which the European funds of the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism are a key starting point. With regard to housing policies and the urban agenda, the importance we attach to them is evident in the budgetary effort they concentrate, with an increase of 373 % over the current budget. Thus, we allocate to housing and urban agenda 2250 million euros, an increase of 1775 million, figures that offer us the largest budget in history in this area. Our priorities will be: on the one hand, to ensure access to housing by increasing the supply of affordable and social rental housing, rental subsidies and investment in rehabilitation as a key element for economic recovery; and on the other hand, the urban and rural agenda in order to promote urban regeneration and renewal to make our cities more efficient, to order the territory by making rational use of the land and to promote the urban economy of proximity. In implementation of these priorities, the key budget items will be allocated to rehabilitation programmes, which have a total of EUR 1661 million, and of this amount, EUR 1550 million will be allocated to economic and social recovery in residential, neighbourhood and housing environments. The aim is to promote rehabilitation as a key element in the reactivation of the construction sector and in the real estate sector and in compliance with European and national energy, climate and digitisation commitments. This is a field of enormous potential given the conditions of antiquity and conservation of the building park, also for its ability to create jobs and improve the quality of life of its inhabitants. A further 111 million will be devoted to the sustainable and digital rehabilitation of public buildings and to architectural rehabilitation. On the other hand, the social housing policy will have 569 million and will have among its most outstanding items the State Housing Plan with 354 million euros, in accordance with the signed with each of the autonomous communities. In this area we want to give the plan a greater social character by enhancing the change of model in housing policy, and to this end we will focus our efforts on facilitating access to housing for those with fewer resources, creating a public housing park for rent and promoting urban rehabilitation and regeneration, with a particular impact on improving energy efficiency and flexibility. We will also promote the Housing Plan with the Affordable Housing Plan and other housing actions, with an additional 215 million. With regard to transport and infrastructure measures, I detail the main items. With regard to transport, allow me to repeat my earlier reference to the reorientation of the Ministry's policies in this area in order to refer to programmes to support the sustainability and digitisation of transport. Its recipients will be companies as well as other public administrations and will have a total of EUR 1 billion. This is a fundamental change in transport policy, which will allow us to allocate EUR 240 million in the first place to support the digitisation and sustainability of the logistics chain, to promote the implementation of intelligent transport systems, digitisation in freight transport companies, alternative fuel charging points, the renewal of the fleets of trucks and railway equipment or the railway compartments and secure car parks for trucks. Secondly, EUR 345 million for the design and implementation of low-emission areas in urban and metropolitan environments for projects such as bicycle lanes, pedestrian routes, dissuasive parking lots on the outskirts of cities or measures to prioritize collective transport. Thirdly, 410 million for the sustainable and digital transformation of urban and metropolitan transport, which will enable projects to be undertaken to transform public transport fleets powered by alternative fuels. Together with these new actions, we must not forget the maintenance of the instruments which have traditionally formed the fundamental part of transport policy. The financing of public service, rail, air and maritime obligations is planned to ensure the viability of these services and will have a budget of more than EUR 1225 million in 2021. Similarly, air and sea ticket bonuses for residents of non- peninsular territories will have a credit of nearly EUR 800 million in 2021 to finance 75% of the price of air and boat tickets. In this way, we guarantee the right of these citizens to enjoy the tickets by paying only 25% of their fare, applied both on trips with the peninsula and on trips between islands. For its part, subsidies to large families in rail, air and maritime transport will have a budget of just over 24 million, intended to finance the discounts that these families benefit from. In addition, subsidies for the transport of goods with the extra-penisular territories will have a budget of more than EUR 77 million, which will minimise the impact and disadvantage of the geographical position of the non-penisular territories with respect to the transport of goods. Finally, I would like to refer to subsidies for land transport. In this area, as a novelty, the nominative subsidies to consortia and metropolitan entities previously included in the budget of the Ministry of Finance are incorporated into the Ministry's budget this year. These are grants to the Madrid Regional Transport Consortium, to the Barcelona Metropolitan Transport Authority, to the Valencia Metropolitan Transport Authority and to the Autonomous Community of the Canary Islands, which are endowed with a total of about 322 million euros. Alongside them, the budget contains another line of grants under competitive conditions, with a credit of EUR 51 million, intended to finance the provision of the urban collective transport service by the municipalities, logically excluding the municipalities affected by the above mentioned nominative subsidies. I would not like to forget in this chapter the slightly more than 106 million euros destined to meet the reductions of tolls on state highways, maintaining those currently in force and promoting the implementation of further reductions with the aim of enhancing their use, especially for heavy vehicles. On the other hand, by making a brief review of the specific modes of transport, it is the railway that leads the increase in investments with an increase of 36,4 % over the current budget. This increase, amounting to EUR 1567 million, is mainly for the modernisation of the network in general and the reduction of travel times. Among the most notable initiatives in this area are Cercanías 2025, which aims to achieve attractive and reliable public transport as a backbone of urban mobility and which will have a budget of 1.5 million, and the initiatives relating to travellers and freight 2030, which will have a total budget of 4370 million euros. They will be used for trans-European transport networks, highlighting the Atlantic and Mediterranean corridors and the TEN-T network as a whole, which are essential for our national transport system, and for intermodality and logistics, as the promotion of intermodality and the modernisation of distribution nodes are decisive factors in the decarbonisation process. For its part, investment in road maintenance and conservation grows by 14.8%, with an increase of 302 million euros to reach 1240 million, allowing to reverse the conservation deficit of recent years and recovering levels prior to 2011. As far as the maritime mode is concerned, investment growth is 27% and has as its priority objectives the improvement of land access and the promotion of intermodality of the port network, as well as the promotion of alternative technologies and energies, all of which include investment in the improvement of our maritime rescue and pollution control system. In the air mode the investment is increased by 41% and will be used to maintain the operation, safety and quality of our airports and to modernise the air navigation systems. Before concluding, ladies and gentlemen, allow me to stress some of the most important aspects of the budgets that I am presenting to you today. It is, as I said at the beginning, the most ambitious budgets of the last ten years, with an increase of 53%, almost 6 billion more, which will contribute to the economic recovery of our country. We have almost EUR 5 billion from the European Union Recovery and Resilience Mechanism for urban rehabilitation and regeneration, sustainable mobility, digitisation, intermodality and logistics. They are budgets that will allocate 2250 million to housing and urban agenda - 373 % growth -, to increase the supply of social housing, to facilitate access to housing, rehabilitation, renovation and regeneration as a key piece also for economic recovery. For the first time, in addition to investing in infrastructure we invest in the drive to sustainable and digital transport with aid programmes of around EUR 1 billion, of which six hundred million will go directly to private transport companies. The rail sector is leading the increase in investments by 36.4%, which will be used as a priority to modernise the network in general and to reduce travel times. Finally, road investment is also increasing by prioritizing conservation with appropriations increasing by 58 %. In short, budgets that, focused on citizens and with a medium and long-term vision, will drive the transformations and priorities that Spain needs to overcome the consequences of the pandemic that we are going through and to do so with a strengthened welfare state. Finally, I would like to express my thanks to those political formations which, during the course of the draft law, have worked on their amendments, made proposals and even - and I am most pleased about this - have expressed their willingness to support these General State Budgets for the next 2021. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

Such oversight would, in any event, be transferred to the later stage of the award process. On the other hand, the other pragmatic way of carrying out the budget of this ministry, we proposed to them a reform that we believe is fundamental to the mobilization of resources by the private sector of our country and is the law of deindexation to improve the attractiveness of public-private collaboration. At this point I would like to point out that the left always looks at this proposal with a bad eye because they believe that it involves the enrichment of private companies at the expense of citizens and, in fact, when there is no public-private collaboration, what happens is that the public sector assumes all the risks in the execution and maintenance of the infrastructure. It is true that Spain has not had good experience with these contractual formulas, but we can learn from the mistakes and balance the assumption of risks and remuneration so that they are fair. Because you, Minister, know well, like me, that the public sector will not be able to undertake the outstanding projects in this country and try to do so only through debt will be a slab that we will leave our children and will have difficulty paying for the next generations. As was foreseeable, to this amendment of the Deindexation Act your party has also voted no. [[Pause.-A cleaning service worker proceeds to disinfect the speaker's rostrum]] Section 18, Education and Vocational Training We move on to the debate in section 18, Ministry of Education and Vocational Training. For the section presentation, I have the floor on behalf of the Government, the Minister for Education and Vocational Training.
ParlaMint-ES_2020-12-01-CD201201.u97	Thank you, Madam President. Good afternoon, ladies and gentlemen. I compare at the plenary session of the Congress to report on the budget of the Ministry of Education and Vocational Training for the year 2021, a budget that has been designed to go beyond the huge impact that the VOCID-19 pandemic is bearing and that lays the foundation for the modernization and transformation of the education system. The budgets that I am presenting to you today reflect an unprecedented budget increase, because in the knowledge society development can only be achieved with more and better education. The budget for the Ministry of Education and Vocational Training amounts to a total of EUR 5697 million, an increase of 139% over the budget with which we started 2020. There had never been an increase of this magnitude. As I indicated in my previous appearance, the exit from this crisis will not be at the expense of education as in previous years. The three political priorities of the ministry that reflect the budgets are: equity, vocational training and digitization. The items directly aimed at ensuring that no one is left behind in the education system, such as scholarships, expansion of children's education and prevention of early school leaving, amount to EUR 2407 million. To strengthen and develop vocational training we will allocate EUR 1628 million, an investment that will lead to more training and better quality for young people, whether they are employed or unemployed. Accelerating the digitization of the education system, for its part, in a constantly changing world, is essential. We will invest 1012 million in technology resources in student centres and for teacher training in digital skills. I'm going to degenerate the most outstanding games. In the area of equity, there is an increase of EUR 515 million which will allow the reform of the state system of scholarships and grants to continue and guarantee the right to study. With this 35% increase compared to the 2020 budget, the total amount reaches EUR 2090 million, the largest scholarship budget so far. This amount corresponds to the budget of the Ministry of Education and Vocational Training 2050 million. Progress is being made in the expansion of places for the first cycle of Child Education, from 0 to 3 years, thanks to an investment of more than 200 million euros in 2021 for the creation of some 21,800 places of public ownership of the first cycle of Child Education, priority 1 and 2 years. In the field of prevention of early school leaving and improvement of educational outcomes, three important actions are collected, with an investment of 155 million euros; 125 million euros in the Program of Guidance, Advance and Enrichment, PROA Plus, for 1800 centers of special educational complexity. A new initiative to prevent dropouts and improve educational outcomes is also being launched, with an investment of €30 million for the creation of 618 units of personal and family support and guidance for students who are vulnerable to education. These units will provide educational or psycho-pedagogical services in their school areas and districts. Finally and equally important, we will invest EUR 600 000 for the design of a new curriculum based on the development and evaluation of key competences, to make it much more flexible and open, and to promote deep learning through active and collaborative methodologies. With regard to the chapter on the digitization of education, I would like once again to highlight the tremendous adaptation that all educational teams have made in educational institutions to meet the challenge of moving from analogue to digital education. In order to deepen and accelerate the digitisation of the education system, with an investment of 1012 million euros, we are launching a plan for the digitisation of education, which will be developed in collaboration with the autonomous communities. It consists of an investment of EUR 150 million for 250 000 additional computer devices which have already started to be tendered and which corresponded to the amount of EUR 500 000; an investment of EUR 827 million for the installation of 236 318 interactive digital classrooms for the development of a hybrid system of face-to-face and distance learning. Another important issue is the digital competences of teachers, for which we will invest 32 million euros in 2021. Vocational training is another of our priorities, as you know well. The budget of the year 2021 for FP reaches 1628 million euros, including regular budget, investments of the Recovery and Resilience Plan and, in addition, the competences of professional training for employment, linked to the national catalogue of professional qualifications, assumed in this legislature. The FP, the vocational training for employment assumed by the Ministry of Education and Vocational Training, has a budget of €974 million. For its part, the vocational training of the education system will reach a budget of 654 million by 2021. Taking into account that it started in January 2020 from a budget of 187 million euros, we note that this figure has experienced an increase of 349 % in one year. I would like to recall at this point that last July the President of the Government presented the Plan for the Modernisation of Vocational Training 2020-2023, which was accompanied by a credit transfer of EUR 316 million by 2020. This plan is now strengthened and increased with the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism. I would like to highlight some of the most important actions, not all of them. One, the accreditation of basic and professional skills, with an investment of 127 million euros. With this initiative we will recognise and accredit in 2021 the skills acquired through the work experience of 532,000 people. Two, increase in the supply of FP places in the education system, with an investment of 64 million euros to create 47 400 new places this year. Three, investment of 170 million euros to start the bilingual conversion of middle and higher education cycles. Four, modular offer in companies, with an investment of 30 million euros for 125 000 workers, employed and unemployed, who will have training itineraries adapted to the specific needs of each person. Fifthly, the creation of a network of centres of excellence in vocational training, with an investment this year that will reach 50 million euros to launch the first twelve centres. We continue to make progress in measures of the Vocational Training Plan for 2021, with 125 projects for the promotion of innovation and knowledge transfer projects, for 15 million; investment of 11.7 million euros for 212 classrooms in applied technology spaces; 550 entrepreneurship classrooms, for 4.7 million euros; 750 Mentor classrooms, for 3 million euros, in those municipalities with less than 3000 inhabitants; we will also train in this chapter 25 000 teachers and, in addition, we will renew twenty new catalogue degrees to cover eleven strategic sectors, with a budget of 200 000 euros. In policy 24, for employment training, the vocational training budget - as I have already mentioned - amounts to EUR 974 million. It should be noted that all this investment will be directed towards modular supply associated with environmental sustainability and to the reskilling and upskilling of employees and the unemployed. It should also be noted that an additional EUR 140 million will be implemented in vocational training for employment, which corresponds to multiannual programmes and which are under the responsibility of the Public State Employment Service, SEPE, for having started this service and the programmes. Before I conclude, I would also like to refer to Ceuta and Melilla. The budget for the management of education in Ceuta and Melilla is increased by 11.7%, more than the previous year, bringing the total amount to 225 million euros. In addition, a new line of work is created jointly with Radiotelevision Española for the implementation of the Educational Program We learn in Clan - which is continuity of We learn in House -, endowed as 1.2 million euros; it will be very useful for children between 6 and 12 years old. In addition, there are external education programmes of the Ministry of Education and Vocational Training, which amount to EUR 118 million to continue the activities of our centres and programmes abroad, benefiting 40,494 pupils in 27 countries. In the area of lifelong learning for teachers, in addition to what I have already mentioned in digitalisation, we will also allocate an amount of EUR 5.8 million through the Intef for lifelong learning activities. Finally, EUR 1.1 million is allocated to international educational evaluation programmes such as Pirls, Piaac or PISA. I conclude my intervention here to explain to you the General Budgets of the State in relation to the competences of our ministry. The education system is facing, like all Spanish society, the enormous challenge of coping with the difficulties brought by COVID-19. This Government has already clearly expressed its commitment to education by earmarking this year 2020 an extraordinary investment of EUR 2 billion from the COVID Fund for the adaptation of educational institutions to health needs and the expansion of the staff of teachers. Similarly, an urgent investment of 230 million euros was made to launch the Education Plan in Digital and thus acquire half a million computer devices for the most vulnerable families. In addition, the funds for reinforcement and support programmes have been expanded, some difficulties - the difficulties experienced by our families and our students - which are also being shown with undeniable evidence and are expressing urgent needs for the modernization of Spanish education and for the improvement of quality with excellence, equity with excellence for the quality of our education system. The unprecedented increase we propose in these budgets places education as a key investment for recovery and transformation. Investment, this investment, is a necessary condition, albeit not sufficient, to have a quality, innovative and transformative educational system. Moving on to a policy of cuts and a retrograde legal framework like the one we have been through these years is an essential first step. We hope to have the broadest support of your ladies and gentlemen at this stage of modernization and enhancement of the education that the young deserve and society demands of us. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]
