ParlaMint-ES_2020-12-01-CD201201.u125	Good afternoon, President. Ladies and gentlemen, what we are bringing to this House today is a proposal for the future, the fruit of a joint and collective effort; they are not mere accounts of one more exercise, but the definite and solidary design of the foundations on which we are going to grow. There is more to these budgets: the commitment of the Government of Spain to quality employment and the final package to an extended 2018 budgets that in no way could already meet the needs that our country faces following the social and economic crisis resulting from the COVID-19. It is an expansive budget, 32,600 million euros, which represents an increase of 22,5%, 5893 million euros more than that budget that we left behind. We leave a budget behind and we also leave behind austerity and cuts. We are moving in the opposite direction: more rights for citizens, better public services and services and a strong boost to economic activity and employment. Europe has historically been built around its fractures, its crises, and the Europe with which this budget dialogues, ladies and gentlemen, is a Europe of one speed, reconstituted over people; a human engine that will drive more just, more egalitarian, more sustainable scenarios, ultimately more in line with our time and the challenges ahead. Allow me to briefly explain the figures and priorities for action in this budget. The Ministry of Labour and Social Economy will make a unique effort in employment to develop the budget lines that will be detailed. Firstly, there are active employment policies, because their redesign and implementation will be the backbone of our ministerial strategy. It is 6481,7 million euros, assuming an increase of 35,75 % over the previous budget, that they will undermine a topic resigned in our productive model, the one that says that only temporarily and precariously it is possible to grow and create employment, that we must choose between the bad and the worse, and that is not the case, we must prove that it is not so. In parallel, we have increased resources for self-employment and the social economy by almost 40%, because of their objective relevance but also because that is where many of the organizational and solidarity responses we need are located. Because the social economy, with its innovative initiatives attached to the territory and society, is in itself a source of quality employment. We will better and better protect the unemployed by strengthening the coverage of the system, which in 2021 will have 24.6 billion euros for contributory benefits and subsidies, 20.1% more in social protection. The crisis has exposed our vulnerability and confirmed that low-income families are the ones who suffer the most from economic inequality and those who have the most difficulty in accessing a job. We will greatly improve the level of protection for the most vulnerable by increasing Iprem by 5%, with an impact on the budget of EUR 550 million, which will, as you know, improve the level of public benefits for more than 1.5 million people. Our policies and their economic expression must curb this inequality, enable new social scenarios of growth for all and for all, all and all, men and women. This is one of the cross-cutting axes in the development of this budget, both at the policy and active policy levels, with programmes aimed at the recruitment of women from particularly vulnerable groups, also through incentives for the recruitment of female workers on the basis of suspensions or surpluses, which has proved to be a powerful mechanism for maintaining the labour market, and with programmes to support employment in rural and urban areas, overcoming gaps and encouraging a sustainable relationship with our work and living spaces. If we turn our backs on precariousness and cuts, as I tell you, we will reach out to our youth, because it will be the one that builds recovery and reconstruction after this crisis. It can't be any other way. They're our talent, they're our chance. These budgets will enable progress to be made in appropriate labour integration policies targeting young women and young people. The tools are set out for innovative treatment of training processes in alternation with work activity. Alongside the development of existing employment programmes, such as youth recruitment incentives or workshop school programmes or youth guarantee programmes, attention is focused on the return of migrant talent and youth employment plans in research programmes. It is necessary to invest in their training because training and talent continue to guarantee employment and opportunities, i.e. a dyke against inequality. This youth, ladies and gentlemen, must also nurture the next process: the Plan of Reconstruction, Transformation and Resilience of the Spanish economy. An ambitious programme that must advance in a new economic model based on quality employment, with trained and qualified people for the productive changes and challenges of digital transformation and the ecological transition that will bring us in the coming years. To this end, the Ministry of Labour and Social Economy participates in the reconstruction fund, as you know, with EUR 1167.5 million. We have the autonomous communities and we will significantly increase the amount of funds transferred to them, so that they can better develop their competences in active employment policies, advancing in a model of shared governance in which the Sectoral Conference on Employment and Social Affairs plays a relevant role. We will strengthen the Labour and Social Security Inspectorate, the fundamental instrument to ensure compliance with the law, which will have a budget of EUR 175.8 million, which will allow us to expand the different categories of the inspection activity into 394 professionals. This budget is clearly committed to the functioning of all the agencies under the Ministry, especially the State Public Employment Service, with an increase in their staff and, above all, with a strong investment in their technological transformation. To this end, Chapter 1 of the SEPE will be increased, in order to be able to continue to count 1500 temporary persons already hired and with an investment of 73,6 million euros additional for its digitisation. The budget for the year 2021 will also allow us to continue the scheme for the protection of ERTE while the evolution of the health crisis makes it necessary. We know that maintaining employment is keeping businesses, as we have proposed, and it allows us to guarantee incomes and protect working people. This European root mechanism has solved the toughest of the crisis, and the internal flexibility it provides will undoubtedly be key to continuing to preserve our country's productive and entrepreneurial fabric in the future. To the future is where the budgets that we present today are headed, because they face our structural legacy of unemployment and precariousness and above all because they challenge it, because they encourage us to rebuild that better normality referred to by ILO Director General Guy Ryder: on the fertile land of decent work; because they link economic reactivation to productive transformation, integrating the change of the energy model, digital modernization, reindustrialisation, at the same time as the demographic challenge or the great challenge of the welfare state of the twenty-first century, the society of care. We need to Europeanize our labour policies and match working conditions with the most advanced countries in our environment. We need reforms just as we need investment, and we need this new generation of active employment policies with a common road map at the hands of the autonomous communities and the social partners. In this regard, it is of particular importance - as I have already advanced - to train people in the labour market. Productive transformations, productive transitions and changes in the world of work will require a huge effort in the field of vocational training. Alongside training, these budgets raise the stake in the digitization of public employment services: the use of artificial intelligence for access routes, for profiling of job search and improvement processes; online services accessible from our own mobile phone; more effective personalised services, together with a huge effort in the area of personnel, stabilizing and expanding the essential staffing of career counsellors and counsellors. With this budget, ladies and gentlemen, we are drafting the foundations of a new social contract that our country needs, a social contract that tells us about cohesion, innovation and inclusive growth. A social contract that speaks to us of equity, that overcomes gaps and decompensations, that speaks to us of a job of quality as a necessary soul of this transformation, of transversal social policies that stop the damage that has produced the process of decomposition of middle incomes and that allows social and labor mobility; that makes us more equal, with more confidence, more feminists, with an intergenerational dialogue that heals the wounds of our society and with the commitment of public accounts such as those that we bring to this House today. ParlaMint-ES_2020-12-01-CD201201.u131	Thank you, Mr. President. Madam Minister, I am going to focus on an amendment that we have developed, a measure that is being controversial these days and that we are proposing together with many other European green forces, which consists of reducing working time to four days a week, to thirty-two hours. We have presented a pilot project of EUR 50 million to study the feasibility of reducing working hours and to accompany companies in their adaptation, without entailing additional costs for them. I have to tell you that I did not understand the Government's opposition, when you yourselves voted in favour of a project like this and that it is under way in the Valencian Generalitat. Why is the reduction of working hours to four days a week good? Firstly, it is a democratic measure. It's not free who doesn't have time. We must have time to be able to participate in politics, to take care of our own and to be able to reconcile and, ultimately, to be able to be a citizen and deal with public affairs. Aristotle already said that only those who have time can think politically and democracy, in short, requires that ordinary people have time. Life can't just go from home to work and from work to home. Secondly, it is a green measure. By reducing displacement, we reduce greenhouse gas emissions. Quieter lives are less polluting lives. Thirdly, it is a health measure. We live in societies that are sick of stress, without time to take care of what we eat, without time to take care of our own, with many people having to take pills to endure the rhythm, with many people to whom, simply the rhythm of life surpasses it. We have to slow down the pace to live better. Finally, it is an economic measure. We must abandon an obsolete mentality, which is fixed only on the number of hours worked. The key to wealth generation is productivity. Look, in 1850, in Spain we worked eleven hours, on average, and in 2015 we worked eight hours, on average, and today the wealth is eight times greater. More wealth, working less. The key is productivity. It is clear that this is a model of the future, of the ecological transition, of the commitment to innovation and of the sharing of work in order to generate more employment. There may be many who continue to distrust and say that if this were done, the economy would sink, that it would be impossible. I don't say his brothers-in-law, but his political and economic ancestors two centuries ago said that the economy would sink if child labour was banned, and it didn't sink. Then, a century ago, they said that the economy was going to sink when the eight hours of work were approved, and not only did it not sink, but that the workers' struggle was an incentive for economic development and innovation. There may be someone who tells us, in good faith, that precisely now is not the time. Well, we think exactly the opposite. Precisely, in reconstruction, it is time to change the model, bet on innovation and share the work to generate employment. Finally, with fewer readings and more cynicism, there have been those who have told us that this is the definitive model to stimulate vagrancy. We affirm that this is a model for freeing time, but that, in fact, it does not have to be a good model for everyone. During this budgetary procedure we were able to see the enormous work of your ladies and gentlemen from VOX by submitting zero amendments to the general budget. Indeed, a working day of thirty-two hours a week would already be a brutal increase in the workload for VOX Members. Thank you very much.

[[Pause.-A cleaning service worker proceeds to disinfect the speaker's rostrum]] Section 20, Industry, Trade and Tourism We continue with the debate in section 20. Ministry of Industry, Commerce and Tourism. For the presentation of the section, I have the floor on behalf of the Government, the Minister for Industry, Trade and Tourism.
ParlaMint-ES_2020-12-01-CD201201.u141	Thank you, President. Good afternoon, ladies and gentlemen. I stand before the plenary of the Congress of Deputies to defend the general lines of the 2021 State General Budgets Bill in section 20, Ministry of Industry, Trade and Tourism. But before I enter the budget guidelines and figures, I would like to refer briefly to the actions of the Government since the start of the COVID-19 pandemic. Our priority has been to protect the health of the population and minimize its impact on the economy and society. And we are doing so by relying on the strength of dialogue, consensus and unity. Since the beginning of the global health crisis, the Government has been responding decisively and ambitiously, adopting successive packages of measures in the economic, labour and social fields, through shock plans, first, and then, with recovery plans in strategic sectors such as automotive, tourism and transport, which have enabled us to maintain a solid basis for better economic recovery. All the measures taken have been effective, because they have cushioned the negative impact of the pandemic on economic activity and employment, but also on society as a whole. However, the priority for the Government is to continue to take all necessary support and stimulus measures as long as the pandemic lasts, in order to avoid a structural deterioration of our productive tissue that puts recovery at risk. Given the high uncertainty surrounding the evolution of the pandemic, the announcement about the effectiveness of vaccines has undoubtedly led to a light at the end of the tunnel. Last week, as you know, the Council of Ministers approved the vaccination schedule that gives us additional certainty in this area, because if something has made clear to us this pandemic is that health and economy go hand in hand, so any progress towards the end of the pandemic will of course also mean progress towards consolidating economic recovery and better expectations for the future. All this, ladies and gentlemen, only reinforces the need to adopt a new budget that is stable for the legislature, that is expansive to support recovery, and that provides us with the tools and resources necessary to be able to respond to these uncertainties, following a solid and prompt economic recovery. That is why these budgets are so important not only to underpin recovery, but also to lay the foundations for the transformation into our economic model towards a more balanced, more inclusive, more sustainable and more digital model. That is why our ministry, the Ministry of Industry, Trade and Tourism, plays a very important role as an instrument for change, according to the three sectoral areas that I have the honour to lead. In this case, industry, trade and tourism not only have a direct impact on economic growth, but are also intensive in terms of employment. I now go on to detail the ministry's budget for 2021, which amounts to 4860 million euros, representing an increase of 2068 million euros, 74% more than the 2018 extended budget. I would like to point out that the non-financial budget has increased by 338 %, to EUR 2396 million, which represents 49 % of the total budget. Ladies and gentlemen, this is the largest public budget in history to directly support the revival of the three areas of my competence, industry, trade and tourism. As you know, the budget incorporates the investments and reforms linked to the recovery and resilience mechanism provided by the European Union. Next, I will explain to you the main areas of spending and the key lines of the ministry, and I will start by talking about the tourism sector. In tourism, the allocation of allocations is provided with a budget of EUR 1350 million for 2021, an increase of EUR 037 million, 331% compared to the 2018 extended budget. Our priority is that Spain remains the most competitive country in the world in terms of tourism and the budget for 2021 is undoubtedly an instrument to advance in the revival of the tourism sector, but also to address the transformation of our tourism model towards a more sustainable, digital, accessible, inclusive and secure model. In the area of sustainability, we will mobilise €683 million in tourism sustainability plans in destination. Through these plans we want to promote a comprehensive recovery of the pioneer destinations and a strategic transformation of the interior urban destinations, as a fundamental complement to our tourist offer. This investment will certainly allow our destinations to compete with better instruments and with higher quality in an increasingly demanding international market. These plans are an example of how we are able to be more effective from public-private collaboration and how tourism is to be, and is, an example of unity. Another area of action in tourism will be digitisation. An allocation of EUR 64 million is planned to enhance the smart tourist destination model and to promote innovation and knowledge in tourism, with the aim of improving our tourist information and intelligence system. A €71 million digital transformation and innovation plan for tourism companies will also be promoted. In short, we are putting a public investment for the next fiscal year of 135 million euros to boost the digitization and intelligence of our tourism model, and thus remain, as I said, leaders in competitiveness. In the area of trade, by 2021 the budget amounts to 524 million euros and represents an increase of almost 1 billion regarding the extended budget of 2018. We will continue to support proximity trade in its recovery from the pandemic and, over the course of 2021, we will promote competitiveness, innovation and the use of new technologies by the commercial sector, promoting their adaptation to new consumption habits and improving the management of their business model, paying particular attention to the SMEs and micro-SMEs of proximity trade. EUR 101 million will be allocated for the modernisation of municipal markets, commercial areas, non-sedentary sales markets and short marketing channels, as well as for the strengthening of commercial activity in areas of tourist influx. We will maintain the agreement with the Spanish Chamber of Commerce to develop the comprehensive plan to support the competitiveness of retail trade, with more than 2 million euros. Another 5 million will go towards the rehabilitation of rural areas, with a special emphasis on addressing the demographic challenge and EUR 500 000 will go to a connected trading platform. In addition, we will strengthen the recognition of the commercial sector through the various awards we award, the small trader's awards, for commercial development and business modernization; the non-profit merchants' awards, for the creation and development of open commercial centres or the prizes for the contest of technological ideas for retail trade. They are a recognition to make visible a trade that is undoubtedly part of our economic activity. A priority for the Government will continue to be the continued momentum towards a greater and better presence of Spanish companies in international markets. In this area, EUR 83 million in transfers to ICEX, including market opening programmes, amounting to EUR 15 million in 2021, include foreign investment in R & D & I activities through the co-financing of EUR 10 million by ICEX-Invest in Spain and the creation of a line for financing technical assistance from the EIF. It is a question of responding to recurrent demand from the private sector and will also be particularly important for the Horizon Africa Strategy. In the area of industry and SMEs, the budget proposal amounts to a total of 2931 million and represents an increase of 927 million over the 2018 extended budget. The Government's priority is to increase the weight of industry in our economy with the conviction that it is a key element for improving our productive model, but also for adapting it to technological and sustainable change, making a decisive contribution to job creation. In 2021, the main developments in the industrial budget are as follows: we will create the fund to support productive industrial investment, with a budget of EUR 600 million to finance industrial investment projects that contribute to strengthening industrial development, strengthening competitiveness in industrial enterprises and maintaining industrial capacities throughout the national territory. We are going to launch a new programme for the industrial transition, with EUR 110 million, and we are going to strengthen the support for the large electro-intensive industry with more than EUR 300 million. The industrial entrepreneurship support programme, with 151 million, strategic development projects in priority industrial sectors and actions for the digital transformation of the industry with 160 million euros, or pioneering projects in technological, electrical, battery and storage innovation and technologies linked to green hydrogen, which will mobilise public investment of 200 million euros. We are also aware that SMEs and entrepreneurs are key players in our economic fabric. Our priority is to improve the environment in which it operates, removing barriers and regulations, improving access to finance, seeking to increase its size, in particular the transition of small and medium-sized enterprises, supporting the search for new markets abroad or encouraging innovation and entrepreneurship. In this area of small and medium-sized enterprises, the refinancing of the guarantees granted by the Mutual Guarantee Society to self-employed persons and SMEs is maintained through a grant to the Spanish Company of Reaffirmation, Cersa, of 123 million euros. Also focused on entrepreneurs is enhanced the granting of loans through the National Innovation Company, Enisa, endowed with 98.5 million euros of budget. And finally, we are going to launch the Spain Entrepreneur Nation Strategy, whose objectives are to promote structural change towards a more entrepreneurial economic model in all sectors, to promote the entrepreneurship and growth of SMEs, to support the development of entrepreneurial capacities and to position Spain as an attractive country for entrepreneurship. I would like to conclude this presentation of the main programmes of section 20 of the 2021 General Budgets, corresponding to the Ministry of Industry, Trade and Tourism, with one final reflection. We are faced with budgets that are, in addition to being necessary, essential; essential for the revival of our economy, essential for modernizing our productive model and, ultimately, essential for making our economy a more sustainable, digital, inclusive and resilient economy. From the Ministry of Industry, Trade and Tourism we will prioritize industrial projects for digitization and technological innovation, the support to the large electrointensive industry, the modernization of commercial and rural trade areas, the support for the digitization and sustainability of the tourism sector and the modernization of areas of tourist influx, not to mention the support to SMEs and their internationalization. We have a historic opportunity with the European Recovery and Resilience Mechanism to address these transformations in the coming years and these budgets are clearly geared to channeling these European funds efficiently. Ladies and gentlemen, public-private collaboration must be the fundamental tool in implementing this country project, so the mechanisms for collaboration must be as agile and efficient as possible. A task of this magnitude cannot be carried out without the cooperation of companies, workers, autonomous communities, local authorities and parliamentary groups. The sooner the 2021 General State Budgets are approved, the sooner we can disburse the public resources needed to mobilize private investment in strategic projects. Although there are many uncertainties, we certainly have reasons for confidence. We know that to give certainty we have to effectively control the pandemic, we have to continue to protect our productive and social fabric, but also to approve these budgets will allow us to implement the great transformations that this country needs. I'm done. If this draft budget comes forward, we will certainly launch an image of confidence and stability abroad that will result in the strength and credibility of Spain. That is why I thank the parliamentary groups that have given their support to these budgets for their commitment and I appeal to the other groups to support with their vote these budgets that will allow us to support the recovery, but above all to transform our productive model and modernise our country. Thank you so much. [[Applauds]] The Vice-President : Thank you very much. [[Pause.-A cleaning service worker proceeds to disinfect the speaker's rostrum]] Section 21, Agriculture, Fisheries and Food We continue with the debate in section 21, Ministry of Agriculture, Fisheries and Food. The Minister for Agriculture, Fisheries and Food, on behalf of the Government, has the floor for the section's presentation.
ParlaMint-ES_2020-12-01-CD201201.u160	Thank you very much, Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, I appear in this House to present the main lines of the draft State General Budgets for the year 2021 with regard to the appropriations entered under Section 21, corresponding to the Ministry of Agriculture, Fisheries and Food. They are budgets designed for a fiscal and budgetary strategy to combat the effects of the economic crisis resulting from the pandemic caused by the COVID-19 and to lay the foundations for the recovery and economic and social transformation of Spain and also the growth of our economy in stronger, more competitive and supportive pillars. These budgets are aimed at promoting the competitiveness, sustainability and profitability of both the agricultural and fisheries sectors and the agri-food industry. Ladies and gentlemen, the agri-food sector in our country is a strategic engine of our economy that is demonstrating its enormous strength despite the difficult situation we are experiencing from the health crisis caused by the coronavirus. As I have had occasion to point out on many occasions in this very rostrum, the Government of Spain, since the beginning of the pandemic, has regarded the agri-food sector as essential and strategic; its response to this difficult situation has been agile, has been effective, legitimising even more the importance of having at all times diverse, safe and quality food at reasonable prices, without forgetting, without doubt, the difficulties arising from the total or partial closure of the Horeca Canal that have affected us greatly, and against which the Government has logically adopted horizontal support measures also specific to the sector, as well as those arising from the European Union. And just as this sector has resisted very well in the crisis, I am convinced that it will play a very important role in economic recovery. The budget I present to you in Section 21 has actions aimed at the development of the rural environment, the consolidation and improvement of irrigation programmes, the regulation of markets, the improvement of the competitiveness and quality of agri-food production, the prevention and improvement of health and the prevention of risks in production, and also food control. In the area of fisheries, the protection and conservation of the sea, participation in the planning of research policy on biodiversity of marine ecosystems, sustainable management and exploitation, protection of fisheries resources, marketing and processing of fisheries resources and research. To all this this section of this budget devotes an overall figure of 8496 million euros, which is 10.47 % more than the previous budget, an amount of 805 million extra euros, including 406.5 from the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism. Obviously, this budget has in its heart and as a fundamental part of its financial daring everything relating to the European Agricultural Guarantee Fund, the EAGGF, as could not be less, and also to the EAFRD, the European Agricultural Fund for Rural Development, for all the actions that have to do with our agriculture, our livestock and our rural environment. There are EUR 7159 million, of which 5793 are dedicated to the first pillar and EUR 1366 million to the second pillar, to rural development policy, which clearly show the involvement of European policy in the management of this ministry. The CAP is certainly the main instrument we have, and I do not want to go into each of the sections, programmes or subprogrammes, but I do want to mention one in particular, which is the Posei, the Programme of Options specific to the remoteness and insularity of the Canary Islands, where last Friday night we finally managed in Brussels to maintain the full support of the EUR 268 million dedicated to this purpose. [[Applauds]] I think it has certainly been a remarkable success that our farmers and the entire rural environment can enjoy. Similarly - and this is the second point I want to make - the EUR 1366 million of Community rural development funds will be increased with national and autonomous co-financing to more than EUR 2 billion. I would also like to mention in this context the third component of the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan dedicated to the environmental and digital transformation of the food and fisheries sector, where we have included eleven investment projects worth EUR 406.5 million. Issues such as water saving or energy efficiency, modernisation of animal and plant health laboratories, improvement of agricultural holdings or support for digitisation or research, innovation and digitisation of the fisheries sector are, of course, key elements that complement all the implementation of the budget with regard to the common European agricultural policy. As far as the breakdown within the ministry is concerned, I have to point out that a fundamental part is certainly in relation to the agriculture and food part, EUR 7681 million, to which 388 of the recovery plan should be added, so 8069 as a global figure in the field, as I said, agriculture and food. As far as fisheries are concerned, a total of EUR 65 481 million is concerned, all of which relate to agricultural and livestock activities, particularly irrigation policy, as well as to the elements linked to rural development and to the specific problems arising from the health problems of agricultural production, the promotion and cooperative integration and the development of our agri-food sector. As regards fisheries, the two programmes, programme 415A and programme 415B, see their amount increased by 42.46 % and 25.15 % respectively and are a clear element of support for the whole of our fisheries sector, which is so important within the whole of our agri-food landscape. I conclude my speech, ladies and gentlemen, by referring to two issues that are particularly dear to me in this budget that I am presenting to you and to this House. The first concerns agricultural insurance, which is a fundamental pillar of the agricultural income of our farmers and farmers and of the viability of our farms. It is a fundamental element and I want to say to you with satisfaction that at this time, and by closing the statistics, as of November 22, this year 369,794 policies have already been subscribed for 347,436 in the same period of 2019, with a much larger insured capital, 13,381 million euros compared to 12,192 million euros in the same period of the previous year. We are therefore going to achieve a new historical ceiling on agricultural insurance, which is obviously a very important element of modernisation and support for business profitability. In this respect, the Government of Spain, to which I am honoured to be a party, has wanted to support the agricultural insurance sector and the whole of the Spanish agricultural sector, including an additional 40 million to increase this figure by EUR 251.2 million. It is the largest increase in agricultural insurance in the last 20 years and that comes to stop the fall that from January 2012 until May 2018 had occurred from 285 million to 211 million, a drop of 74 million that in a single year we will recover in more than half thanks to this budget. This will therefore enable us to subsidise all actions relating to cases of policies with a high degree of disaster where, unfortunately, they obviously need additional support in relation to climatic contingencies, the additional subsidy for young farmers, the additional subsidies for losses of extensive livestock pastures or also the supplementary insurance which will be the subject of additional support. I conclude by referring to another issue that I am particularly fond of, because it also shows the competitiveness, modernisation and innovation of our entire primary sector, which concerns the renewal of agricultural machinery, for which we have included in this budget EUR 10 650 000, which represents a very significant increase of almost a third over the previous one established in the extended budgets of 2019 and which, for the first time, means the inclusion not only of a direct subsidy, but also of a possibility, a line created within that EAEC for credit guarantees, which will allow us to cover the purchase of machinery up to an additional EUR 30 million, taking into account the classical exhaustion of these lines, given the success of these lines. I conclude, Madam President, ladies and gentlemen. These budgets, as far as Section 21 is concerned, constitute a powerful instrument and a solid instrument in support of our farmers, our farmers, our fishermen and our rural environment, all of which will ultimately bring about the recovery and transformation that we want for the Spanish economy as a whole and also particularly for our agri-food sector, of which we are so proud. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2020-12-01-CD201201.u180	Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, good afternoon. I compare with all of you to share the general budget lines of the Ministry of Territorial Policy and Public Service, section 22, for the financial year 2021. The figures I am now going to comment on are part of country budgets, progressive and transformative budgets that seek to overcome the health crisis, strengthen the welfare state and represent a fantastic opportunity to achieve the economic and social changes that allow our country to move forward, to reactivate the economy and, at the same time, to lay the foundations for the change of a productive model. The Ministry of Territorial Policy and Public Service, which I have the honour to lead, has two major objectives in two major areas. On the one hand, to contribute to territorial cohesion, by enhancing the policies of cooperation of the State with the autonomous communities and local authorities, and on the other hand, in the field of public service, the proper functioning and modernization of public administrations. To this end, the Ministry has 10 budget programmes, of which five are implemented directly by the department's governing bodies, four by its autonomous agencies and one is jointly managed. The total consolidated budget for the year 2021 of the ministry and of the autonomous bodies attached to it - that is, the INAP, Muface and the Transparency and Good Government Council - amounts to the amount of 2509 million euros, 338 million more, ladies and gentlemen, which means an increase of 15.57 %. Analyzing the State subsector, i.e. exclusively section 22, without therefore including the autonomous agencies under our ministry, the budget for the year 2021 amounts to 619 million euros. Compared to the base budget for 2020, we have an increase of EUR 263 million, which, as a percentage, means an increase of 74% in the budget. Much of this increase, ladies and gentlemen, is due to the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan presented by the President of the Government on 7 October, which is the mechanism through which the implementation of the funds should be guided to receive the European Recovery Fund for the years 2021-2023. This plan, as you know well, is structured around ten lever policies, ten tractor policies, which will directly affect those productive sectors with the greatest capacity to transform our country, our economic and social fabric. The fourth lever policy, the fourth tractor policy, aims to promote a public administration for the twenty-first century, so that the public administration can act as an engine for technological change, which includes a plan to modernize our public administrations through the digitization of services, and the improvement and efficiency of all public services. Ladies and gentlemen, for the entire period 2021-2023, the ministry will count on EUR 600 million, distributed as follows: EUR 570 million, i.e. 95%, will go to autonomous communities -EUR 340 million - and local authorities -EUR 230 million; and EUR 30 million, i.e. the remaining 5%, directly to the Ministry of Territorial Policy and Public Service to develop the lines of action I am going to comment on throughout this intervention. This plan has been distributed by annuities, so that a substantial part of the financial effort is made in the first two years, in the years 2021 and 2022, and will account for 80% of the expenditure, with only 20% remaining for the year 2023. We have created a programme, the 920.A, aimed at hosting these funds, which is structured for the year 2021 as follows: 11.8 million for such actions in the ministry itself and 228 million for public administrations, of which 92 million euros are for local entities and 136 million euros are for autonomous communities. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, 95% - I insist - of the credits of the MRR, the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism, go to processes of modernization of autonomous communities - 56,7% - and to local entities - 38,3%. As far as the area of territorial policy is concerned, ladies and gentlemen, I would quickly like to comment that programme 922.M, the territorial organization of the State and the development of its systems of collaboration, which seeks to promote and develop inter-administrative collaboration between the central, the autonomous and the local administrations, monitored the system of distribution of competences and is equipped for this exercise 2021 with 10.33 million euros, which refers mainly to the increase in Chapter 1. The 942.A programme, of local economic cooperation of the State, is a programme that promotes the system of cooperation between the AGE and local entities, through its participation in different lines of action, and provides specific resources for local entities. This programme is endowed with 35 million euros, and highlights - I want to mean it - the transfer to the Spanish Federation of Municipalities, which is increasing by 5%, and the Investment Fund of Teruel, the FITE, with 30 million euros destined to finance as a means of cooperation to the economic development of areas with special difficulty, as is the case of Teruel. For its part, programme 921.P, of general administration in the territory - a new programme - aims to improve the organization and functioning of the AGE in the territory; in particular, it seeks to provide the delegations and sub-delegations of the Government with the personal and material means necessary to guarantee the quality of the public services we provide in the administrations. It experiences a slight increase of 2.7%, which is mainly intended to meet the public employment offers of the years 2018 and 2019, which cover government delegations and sub-delegations, and also a slight increase in Chapter 2, with EUR 1.8 million to provide PPE equipment for the protection of the staff of these services, so that they can provide them with the best guarantees. I would also like to highlight a slight increase in Chapter 6 for investment and technological improvement in government delegations and sub-delegations dealing with the improvement of services provided to citizens, such as foreign offices, vaccination centres, coordination units against violence against women and PIFs, border inspection posts. In addition, ladies and gentlemen, we have strengthened the delegations and sub-delegations of the Government with €7 million to strengthen their work, which we think is tremendously important. Ladies and gentlemen, in the area of Public Service, I highlight the 0.9% increase for public employees, and above all the Plan for the Modernisation of Public Administrations, on which the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism, the MRR, will be piloting on the following vectors: the digital modernisation of human resources management tools; the drive for the digitisation of procedures and the strengthening of electronic registers; the development of a technological platform for citizen care and for improving the accessibility of the transparency portal of the General Administration of the State; the drive for open government policy in the digital environment; the development of a digital talent attraction programme; actions to improve decentralization and telematisation of selective processes in innovation, the digitisation of learning and training, and the design and implementation of a training plan for teleworking. Also, ladies and gentlemen, it will be an important axis of this modernisation plan for learning and information innovation and, finally, a training plan for public employees with new technologies. Ladies and gentlemen, I also highlight programme 921.N, which aims to boost public governance and open government and, of course, for the new plan that we have adopted, the Fourth Open Government Plan, a tremendously important, very significant plan that has the necessary budgetary momentum to carry it out. The OIC, the Office for Conflict of Interest, is also going to increase by 5.33% to finance these actions, which seem to us to be extremely relevant and important. Ladies and gentlemen, I continue with the National Institute of Public Administration. Programme 921.A is intended to exercise the powers of INAP for the training and development of public employees, their selection and promotion of the various activities of cooperation, coordination and research and publications. It is endowed with 96 million and an increase of 9.32%, which is meant in two sections. First, in chapter 1, with more than €3 million, to deal with the payroll of new officials and women in practice as a result of the 2018 and 2019 OPEs, tremendously important for the rejuvenation of our staff and the endowment to our General Administration of the State. And, secondly, chapter 2, to deal with selective oppositions and evidence as a result of the agreement we have signed with UNED for the decentralization of selective processes. You know that opponents can present themselves anywhere in our country in the territorial centers of the National University of Distance Education and do not have to come to Madrid for tests. Ladies and gentlemen, I continue with Muface, mutualism, with two programmes. First, 222.M, for economic benefits and administrative mutualism. They know that Muface manages the expenses arising from the protection that must be given to mutualists and their relatives or assimilated: situations of temporary disability, risk during pregnancy, risk during breastfeeding, great disability, retirement, death and so many others, ladies and gentlemen. It has an increase of 8% and highlights the rise in Chapter 4 as a result of the increase aimed at temporary disability, high disability care and functional diversity. Ladies and gentlemen, programme 312.E, also from Muface, for health care for administrative mutualism. This programme covers the costs arising from the provision of health care to officials of the State Civil Administration, their families and their dependants by those medical, surgical or pharmaceutical services. It is endowed with an important amount, ladies and gentlemen, with 1511 million euros and represents an increase of 42 million, which has to do with the increase of transfers to the pharmacy offices and also to the assistance concert with the mutualists. Muface will also undertake, within the Plan Spain Puede, a modernization plan to enhance Muface's communication with mutualists, update its technological infrastructures and the implementation of the digital clinical history of mutualists. Ladies and gentlemen, this is a programme I have tried to summarise briefly at this time, but I conclude by saying to all of you that it is a budget to increase public confidence in the public, in the value of the public, to contribute to the improvement of public administrations through unprecedented modernisation in our public administrations, to give effect to people's rights and thus improve their living conditions. It is a budget that gives us country horizons and gives us hope to look to the future. Thank you very much, ladies and gentlemen. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2020-12-01-CD201201.u198	Thank you, President. Good afternoon, ladies and gentlemen, I appear today to defend sections 10, 15 and 38 of the General Budgets of the State. They correspond to the Centralized Contract, the Ministry of Finance and the Territorial Financing System, respectively. However, first of all, I would like to appeal once again to the sense of responsibility of all your ladies and gentlemen, to the generosity and ability to look up to understand that we are at an extraordinary time which therefore requires extraordinary answers. It is time to show what solutions each of the political formations present in this House poses so that we can all overcome the worst economic and social crisis in our recent history. That is why I would like to thank all the groups that have advanced their readiness to support these public accounts, overcoming sterile debates, far removed from the reality and needs of citizens. Ladies and gentlemen, we have already said that giving the affirmative vote to these budgets is not synonymous with supporting the government or sharing its political project. In fact, it is reasonable that budget projects have external support, at least that is what has been done throughout history, primarily when there has been no absolute majority. It is good that this is the case to enrich the content and obtain greater social and political support. Because that's what this is about, ladies and gentlemen. In short, what we want is to have a willingness to add sensitivities, all of them different, and a capacity for dialogue and consensus to agree on where the country has to walk, generating stability and, in turn, trust and, above all, prioritizing the general interest. We have always done so when we have faced crucial moments and, without a doubt, this is so. It is precisely this Sunday that we will celebrate here in this House the anniversary of the referendum on the Constitution, which was born of a broad consensus among political representatives four decades ago. A consensus on the need to agree on the fundamental norm of coexistence that protects our rights and freedoms and that allows us to overcome a stage of division and backwardness and face a future of unity and progress. And now, ladies and gentlemen, we are at an equally decisive moment because, unfortunately, Spain as a whole is facing a very difficult situation after the outbreak of this pandemic. The answer is not in accounts that were designed three years ago, specifically in 2018. Therefore, supporting budgets at this time is a public service to citizens, to our country, to a country that we represent each and every one of us. On the other hand, to say is not to ignore the reality and needs of millions of compatriots. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, let us push these accounts forward for economic recovery and also to share the importance of strengthening health, education or dependence; ultimately, of shielding the welfare state. We need to be able to take this opportunity to reactivate the sectors most affected by this crisis, accelerating the major transformations that we have been facing for a long time. Now we face an unprecedented moment to be able to approach them and capillaryize them to the whole territory. I enter, ladies and gentlemen, into the contents of the sections and inform you, first of all, of the Ministry of Finance. The ministry's non-financial expenditure for 2021 amounts to 2719 million, 145 more than the initial homogenized budget for 2020. Chapter 1 represents 351 million, an additional 4.30 %, in order to respond, on the one hand, to the updating of the salaries of the Ministry's staff and its seconded agencies and, on the other, to the incorporation of officials from the public employment offer for the years 2018 and 2019. Chapter 2 amounts to 80 million, with an increase of more than 8 million. This heading covers both the actions planned for the charge to the AIReF with regard to the tax monitoring rate and for the review of public expenditure, so that in 2021 the reports that result in the efficiency of this expenditure are prepared. There are also measures planned to improve the Economic and Financial Information Centre. The current transfers chapter has an allocation of 2076 million, an additional 5.8%, being quantitatively the chapter in the budget of expenditure that increases the most. In this section, it would highlight the 95 million increase in the transfer to the Spanish Radio and Television Corporation with the aim of compensating the public service as a result of the issuance of the Tokyo Olympic Games, as well as the compensation for changes in VAT regulations and for salary revisions resulting from the 2018 agreement between the Government and trade unions for the improvement of public employment. The budgetary appropriations to cover expenses arising from the State's responsibilities are provided with 500 million, a figure similar to that reflected in the previous accounts. With regard to internal current transfers, they are intended to finance the operating costs of the State Mobile Park, the Institute of Fiscal Studies and the Tax Agency. It is precisely about the Tax Agency that I would like to highlight that the transfer of the ministry is increasing by 1.77% from the core budget, which shows the commitment of this Executive to the extraordinary work of the agency in the voluntary fulfilment of tax obligations, but especially in the fight against tax fraud and customs fraud. [[The lady]] In relation to actual investments, it is necessary to highlight the items for the acquisition and renovation of computer equipment and the projects of impulse to the electronic administration, with about 30.5 million euros, assuming an increase of just over 4 million. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, it is a question of continuing to adapt daily activity to the non-faced working model, so that the most vulnerable groups can continue to carry out their usual work without having to travel to the office, ensuring in any case the accessibility of the citizens to the services and to the efforts. We have already made an important effort this year, and we will be following the one that has been betting on the digitisation of the administration and the technological renewal of the systems, especially, ladies and gentlemen, those related to the management of European funds. This is as a result of the 27 billion package that feeds these items, which, as you will understand, is important to absorb and therefore monitor their implementation. As for State Heritage, rehabilitation works will be carried out in the former branch of the Banco de España in Soria, in the building of the Plaza del Marqués de Salamanca, in Madrid, and construction works will be undertaken for a new administrative building in Cadiz to concentrate different units and save the leases. Similarly, credit for the contracting portal is increased to a total of 4.5 million with the aim of improving this system to allow the aggregation of public information in different portals or concertation platforms, to increase the transparency of this portal. Other actions under chapter 6 include the technological modernization of the Central Economic and Administrative Tribunal and the Independent Office for the Regulation and Supervision of Procurement. For its part, Chapter 7 highlights a credit of 12 million to meet the share that corresponds to the AGE according to the statutes of the Consortium Valencia 2007. It also provides for the budgets of regular funding funds for the territorial entities with the novelty of a new compartment, which is endowed with 8 billion to provide the communities with funding bridge for the implementation of the operational programmes associated with the European Union RIAD until the funds of the Union are received after certification. At this point, ladies and gentlemen, let me highlight this Government's firm commitment to our territorial and competitive model. Ladies and gentlemen, the pandemic has undoubtedly been a huge challenge for the autonomous state, with its successes and difficulties, because it has been necessary to fatten cooperation mechanisms, to invent and innovate in those of co-governance, which have been unprecedented and we have been learning in our own way. But above all, there is no doubt about the Government's support in these months for the territories. I can literally tell you that we have turned to ensuring that the territorial entities have sufficient funding to enable them to continue to provide the essential public services that are the pillars of the welfare state. They know that the pandemic has caused these territorial entities not only to bear greater pressure on spending, but also to receive less income from the brake on economic activity. To alleviate this situation, the Government has put in place a set of measures that have no precedent in our democratic history. We have updated the deliveries on account, the tax rules have been suspended, an unconditional extraordinary fund has been activated with 16 billion, which has meant the greater transfer of resources from the Government to the autonomous communities, on the margins or in parallel with their financing system. Overall, by 2020, autonomous communities have increased their resources by almost 22%, thanks to this update of both deliveries and the COVID Fund. By 2021 these record figures will be exceeded, reaching a financing of 146 008 million. In particular, the resources of the financing system will reach 113 729 million by 2021, a figure almost similar to 2020 and will therefore alleviate these financing elements. Secondly, the autonomous communities will have an additional transfer, outside this system, of 13,486 million, which the Government has already included in the draft budgets. Thirdly, the communities will have access to 18,793 million funds from the European Union, of which 10,793 million are from the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism and 8000 from the RIAD Fund, which will be directly integrated into the autonomous budgets. For our part, we from the Government argue that local entities are called upon to have a special role in recovery. This is why resources in the budgets increase by 9.5 % compared to 2020. On the one hand, the total amount of State tax participation of local entities amounts to 19,931 million, 1.9% more than in 2020. On the other hand, they will have access to 1483 million from European funds. Although, ladies and gentlemen, perhaps the most important measure for these entities is the possibility of using the remnants, a historic demand that can be met once the Stability Pact clause in force for the years 2020 and 2021 has been activated. Together with this, we will maintain the extraordinary funding mechanisms articulated through the Local Entities Financing Fund, with a budget of 1 billion as in previous years, articulating support measures for those entities with special financial imbalances. I conclude my speech by making a brief reference to section 10, which is dedicated to centralized recruitment with 380 million. The aim is to improve centralized recruitment processes, streamline management and thereby achieve greater efficiency in public spending with significant savings as a result of this aggregate demand. Under this heading, $70 million is earmarked for the renovation of the mobile fleet to incorporate non-emission vehicles. We contribute in this way to improving environmental sustainability, but in turn to boosting and supporting the automotive industry, as reflected in the shock plan presented by the President of the Government just a week ago. I repeat, ladies and gentlemen, this initial idea to finish my words. This is an exceptional budget for a country that now needs new solutions. Please do not make the reading easy in terms of support or not for this Government. These are country accounts that can ostensibly help us through a very important crisis, the most important of recent years, and get out of it in better conditions. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, I would ask you to broaden the focus, the objective of these budgets is not who supports them, but what the accounts provide to solve the problems of citizens, to give them certainty and to provide opportunities, which is what is expected of the political leaders we are here representing them. They are still in time to reconsider their position, do so, ladies and gentlemen, show that they are up to what the country needs and show that the general interest is above any other partisan interest. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

This report should be entrusted to a committee composed of independent experts. Different studies estimate the weight of the economy submerged in Spain at around 25% of the gross domestic product, a figure significantly higher than that of other countries in our environment. Spain suffers from 65 % of the economy that is submerged more than the European average and it is estimated that because of it our public coffers stop entering 90 million euros. If we could officially quantify the submerged economy, we could design a medium- and long-term strategy to reduce it, and it would certainly be a great boost to the continuous improvement in assessing the efficiency of the Tax Agency according to the level of collection of the submerged economy achieved in a temporary period. Another amendment we want to try to make is to increase the staffing of the Tax Agency. Spain holds the penultimate position in the European ranking of the number of employees in the European tax administrations in relation to the total population and the labour force, according to the latest OECD tax administrations report. Each tax employee in Spain has a burden of between 632 and 706 citizens respectively more than the weighted average of the European Union and the euro area, but with the data of January 2019, 15.8% of tax agency employees are very close to retirement and more than half of the workforce, 54.9%, is between fifty and sixty years old. This ageing situation of the staff of the State Financial and Tax Administration places Spain in the penultimate position in the senior ranking among the twenty-three countries of the European Union that have such information. It would be very interesting if these people, who already have experience in the administration of the Tax Agency, had time to delegate and teach people to come to those posts later. All this knowledge acquired during the years of service delivery within the Administration must be used. Spain would have to have between 15 000 and 17 500 new troops to halve the size of the economy submerged in Spain. This requires annual calls gradually for eight or ten years to ensure equal, merit and capacity access to the Public Service; calls that are required to improve the public service. Finally, we also wanted to highlight an amendment with which we want to end the dispute over the tax on economic activities to which the large specialised areas must benefit. Our amendment is to amend it, because the survival of these large areas of specialized sales will be very difficult in these places, in places where their activity is almost strategic because they give employment to a lot of people in populations with fewer inhabitants but where much more employment is needed. As you see, ladies and gentlemen of the Government, we do not propose intractable things, we do not ask for ideological counterparties or scandalous measures. [[The Lord]] This section also includes a substantial increase in the budget belonging to the Tax Agency, which increases by 8.4% to 16 billion. If we take into account that the state loses around EUR 40 billion a year because of tax fraud, we can conclude that there is no better investment than this one we are talking about, not only for quantitative or collective purposes, but also because of the powerful cultural message behind this measure: whoever disappoints does not disappoint the minister or the agency, disappoints all the Spanish and Spanish. The work of the agency and its workers will be fundamental in the coming years, of which I have no doubt, because the effectiveness of the agency is a sine qua non condition for economic recovery not to take place, as always, at the expense of the weakest, for all of us to bow our shoulders according to our capabilities, thus equalizing efforts. But also from my group we think that another of the fundamental elements to optimize the resources allocated to this task would be the creation of a superior technical body of Finance, in which would be integrated the different technical bodies that today operate in the ministry. Unfortunately, it is not something we have yet been able to agree on, but we are confident that we will soon convince the ministry of the goodness of this measure. Finally, with regard to section 38, on the financing of territorial entities, the increase in the budget allocation by 4.6%, to almost EUR 40 billion, should be highlighted. In any case, transfers received from the State by the territorial authorities are not exhausted by those made from credits under this section, as they also receive transfers from other sections, such as 37, other financial relations with territorial authorities, or 36, inter-territorial compensation funds. In short, between the financing system, the extraordinary transfers and the management of half of the European funds, the communities will receive a total of 146 billion euros next year, which is a historic maximum for an extraordinary moment. Ladies and gentlemen, let us see these resources not as a triumph, but as an opportunity, an opportunity that opens up for us to get out of the stumbling block that we are in. Let's be smart and take advantage of it. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]
